# Nested Objects improvement program and Issue #318 canonical execution ledger

Last updated: 2026-09-11, America/New_York

Last audited repository commit: `851cbbb7f4ff6a967db58279c6813c4385799308` (2026-09-11 source/release audit; historical acceptance retains its own SHA/date)

Canonical file: `docs/intelligence-os/issue-318-foundation-execution-ledger.md`

This file is the single canonical implementation plan, status ledger, decision record, and handoff for the full Nested Objects improvement program and GitHub Issue #318. It governs member and subscription truth, ActiveCampaign, acquisition and lifecycle journeys, Free/Pro/Elite value, opportunity intelligence, SEO/AEO/CRO, the Intelligence OS control plane, agents, workflows, deployments, and consequential-action boundaries. Chat history, pull-request titles, deployments, and historical plans are evidence inputs, not competing sources of current status.

Current status must be updated here in the same pull request as any in-scope implementation. Historical evidence remains below and in linked files; it is not silently rewritten or deleted.

## Plain-English program truth

### What Nested Objects is trying to accomplish

Nested Objects is building an inspector-first membership business that helps field inspectors reach useful outcomes: understand the work, find credible firms and opportunities, operate profitable routes, and know when a paid plan creates real additional value. Behind that experience, one operating system should reconcile identity, membership, billing, behavior, marketing, opportunity, and search data so Autumn can see what is true, approve consequential actions, and improve conversion without misleading promises or uncontrolled automation.

### What has been implemented in plain English

The member site now has clearer Free-offer language, a three-firm Free preview, direct Free signup, an onboarding guide, owner-only member profiles, and a private conversion-event ledger. The Intelligence OS has a separate runtime, durable staging tables, deterministic specialist logic, three operating-review workflows, read-only sensor adapters, an owner-only review screen, duplicate-run protection, approval/rejection records, and trace links. Synthetic Preview acceptance proved the main review path without using real member data, models, email, or external mutations. PR #347 is merged and deployed; it hardens rate-limit/contact failure truth, removes embedded Supabase credentials from 34 tracked maintenance scripts, and restores two conservative browser-only calculators. PR #348 is also merged and deployed to the member-site Production project: Free receives only Income Scenarios, Pro receives its established core tool set, and Elite receives every current member tool. Autumn confirmed the Elite defect is fixed. PR #349 is merged and deployed; plan names use the central Outseta UID map and missing live claims fail truthfully instead of displaying stale projection text. PR #350 is merged and Production-live: member dependencies now install once in 15 seconds and the deployment completed in about two minutes instead of about 22 minutes. The first bounded Production source-health readback also established three exact facts: current Upstash decisions are working, the contact-message table was absent (repaired under explicit approval on 2026-09-06), and the scheduled Adzuna collector was fresh but unsafe on partial failure at the September 4 observation. The fail-closed repair subsequently merged in #351 (`6136c16`); that merge does not establish a fresh collector run or legacy-row cleanup. The repaired Production storage passed server receipt and browser-denial checks with a rolled-back synthetic message. On 2026-09-08, PR #354's direct Google contact routing passed the separately approved live test: one saved synthetic receipt, matching inbox notification, and correct visitor Reply-To. Sending is enabled; no reply was sent.

The `OS-005` adapter converts supplied Income Scenarios receipts into evidence for the correct member and subscription. The separate `adaptFreeOnboardingCompletion` in `free-onboarding-evidence.ts` now combines the receipt with observed saved-profile basics, city/state, experience and inspection interests. Both reject incomplete, stale, ambiguous or wrong-cycle evidence. They are locally tested pure adapters; no Production evidence writer, operational read wiring or member journey execution is established. Calculation completion alone does not graduate onboarding or authorize email.

On September 9, #357 passed its approved owner-review acceptance at `6644f31`: the quiet baseline, substantive four-specialist review and fresh repeat all finished on Vercel Preview. The specialist review saved evidence, three priorities and one decision prompt in staging. Repeating it left both saved business reviews and their artifacts unchanged. No real member data, model, email or external action was involved; the Production member experience did not change.

The next revenue increment is now tested locally at `f39db43`: it distinguishes money captured by Stripe from an Outseta subscription, handles refunds and uncertain evidence, and prevents the same Agency payment from being counted once per inspector. It can feed the Revenue Agent with normalized evidence, but it is not connected to live billing data. It does not claim bank payouts, recurring revenue or upgrades from charges. No member-facing behavior changed.

At `f69cb3c`, locally tested read-only connectors can request specific approved billing/account records and discard contact details from their output. A snapshot selector prevents older or duplicate observations from inflating totals. These additions passed mocked tests only; live reads remain disabled, and an individual payment sample is not presented as a complete day of revenue.

On September 10, the separately approved one-account test check passed from a local process at `95489e4`: Outseta identified one test account, the exact linked Stripe test customer matched, and the existing readers accepted its account and one paid/captured test payment. The selected alias currently has **Elite**, confirmed by Outseta and the app's plan map; its name does not prove Pro access. No subscription, member access, email, database or deployed connection changed. This is a provider test-read result, not live-business revenue or hosted agent acceptance.

The reader-to-agent connection at `ebca89d` passed **189 Runtime tests**: an invented provider response travels through the real read-only reader, the new evidence bridge, the existing adapter and snapshot selector, and the Revenue Agent. Fifteen new tests cover this path. A successful individual read still cannot turn into a complete day of revenue; unavailable facts remain unknown, test/live scopes remain separate, and duplicates cannot add a second assessment. No additional member records were read and nothing was enabled in Production.

### What has changed for members

Visitors are no longer promised broad Free directory search. Free members see at most three actionable firm records and locked/sanitized previews beyond that. New inspectors can register directly and receive a first-use guide. Inspector profile browsing is restricted so a member sees only their own profile, and the login regression introduced during those access changes was repaired. Current Production contains PR #348's shared catalog/server authorization policy: Free gets Income Scenarios only; Pro gets client/vendor tracking, company tracking, AI Concierge, AI Resume, job tracking, weather, and route planning in addition to Income; Elite and existing Agency members get all nine tools, including Route Economics; legacy Starter/Founders retain their promised tracking and AI tools. Autumn confirmed the Elite tools are fixed. A controlled signed-in Founders session showed exactly the legacy six-tool subset before #349, and a post-#349 signed-in Elite session showed `Elite Plan` plus all nine enabled tool cards. The exact Founders label after deployment, Pro, Agency, and connected-provider success paths are not yet live-verified.

### What has changed behind the scenes

The September 11 DATA-002 correction stops the Intelligence OS from calling cached profile data verified Outseta subscription truth. It retains the observed plan as a mirror, reports missing verification and withholds subscription-based repair claims. #360 merged as `9916eb7` and its release records succeeded; correction behavior remains accepted through local tests only. No hosted source or historical database repair was verified. Member login, plan/access rules, billing and AC consent remain separate. Old stored records still need a separately scoped inspection before operational consumers can trust them.

Nested Objects now has staging schemas for canonical members, daily metrics, signals, experiments, runs, tasks, actions, events, workflow steps, operating reviews, sensor observations, approvals, outcomes, measurements, and learnings. The runtime can persist synthetic workflows durably, reuse a business key, keep healthy reviews quiet, limit weekly priorities, and show Autumn an approval surface. Conversion events are server-owned and browser grants were removed. The broader Outseta, Supabase, ActiveCampaign, analytics, and billing truth is still fragmented and must be reconciled rather than assumed.

### Which agents are real versus extension contracts

The Operations Orchestrator, Revenue Agent, Growth Agent, Industry Intelligence Agent, and Marketing Agent contain real deterministic logic. On September 9, all four specialists produced nonempty synthetic analysis through the authenticated Preview and saved it in the dedicated staging database: Revenue preserved unknown MRR, Growth compared four time windows, Industry retained a dated invented finding and its permission caveat, and Marketing proposed an audience, experiment and internal copy without sending anything. This is substantive synthetic Preview proof, not live business intelligence. Opportunity now has a deterministic, fixture-tested implementation for exact-source normalization, eligibility withholding, internal copy and an unscheduled-Draft proposal. Its opt-in local review registration is not an accepted durable collector or hosted delivery workflow. Inbox/Member Success, SEO/AEO, Lead/Firm Acquisition, Product, and Engineering/Codex remain extension contracts only. A guarded OpenAI adapter exists, but no agent has model-enabled verification.

The latest checked main is `851cbbb7f4ff6a967db58279c6813c4385799308`, published after #360. On 2026-09-11 its four exact-commit Vercel contexts reported success: Firms 18:37:04Z, Public 18:37:19Z, Runtime 18:37:59Z and Members 18:38:13Z. GitHub deployment records label Members/Public/Firms Production and Runtime Preview. #360 itself merged as `9916eb7e75f274f62d38c6b4627e0b9668802f3e` at 18:21:32Z and also has four successful deployment records. These are release facts, not fresh signed-in/provider tests, error-log scans, opportunity collection, source-connected agent acceptance or Runtime Production enablement.

Earlier functional acceptance remains dated: #348/#349 tool-policy and Elite-label observations; #354 contact-mail activation at `275986f` with one synthetic saved receipt, inbox arrival and visitor Reply-To on September 8; #356 cache-free build repair at `87e23e2`; and #357 substantive synthetic owner-review acceptance at `6644f31` on September 9. Their complete records remain below. None was repeated during this documentation audit.

### What is staging-only

The Issue #318 database/control-plane migrations, one owner registration, destination binding, synthetic workflow rows, approval/rejection evidence, and correlation evidence are in the dedicated Supabase staging project only. Real-member data, live business-source persistence, and Production control-plane migrations remain outside approval.

### What is Preview-only

Current #357 evidence (2026-09-09 UTC, `6644f31`): authenticated owner controls completed baseline, specialist-review-v1 and a fresh repeat. All three Vercel provider deliveries were Completed; the dedicated staging database independently confirmed saved nonempty specialist evidence and unchanged business artifacts after repeat. This advances the specialist paths beyond the older empty-input Preview checks. It does not enable the runtime in Production or prove a live non-owner/replay test.

Earlier dated Preview evidence: the protected Intelligence OS admin experience and durable runtime were accepted with synthetic inputs. Four application runs were verified: conversion review, daily business health, weekly operating review, and a representative integration-failure event. PR #347's member Preview verified its two-tool interim catalog, signed-out login redirect, disabled Weather boundary, and paused Agency purchase before that change merged. PR #348's expanded access matrix passed its hosted checks and Preview build before Production deployment. Before its merge, PR #349 passed all three GitHub validations and all four Vercel Preview builds at `6d6c5e6`. Its authenticated Preview label acceptance remained unverified because hosted Outseta login returned the controlled browser to the configured Production URL, not the Preview origin; the signed-in Production Elite account correctly showed `Elite Plan`, but that is only a baseline observation.

### What is not connected yet

Production Gmail and reviewed-work-chat collection, durable opportunity dispatch, exact-recipient AC Draft transport, and the four separate Draft/notification/approval/send receipts are not operational. Opportunity source/agent/local review code exists, but has no hosted or provider-write acceptance. AC confirms zero opportunity campaigns, owner notifications and sends. The AC owner's corrected bounded audit covers 196/196 profiles, 190 unique stable links and 172 verified exact Outseta accounts; 14 throttled and 4 not-found lookups remain unavailable. Two current non-demo active Elite accounts had exact AC links and active list-12 relations with no observed bounce/unsubscribe. One internal/test identity is excluded; one potential real member has unknown affirmative opportunity-email consent, leaving zero eligible verified candidates. Unavailable is not nonmember, and list status or opaque source ID 4 is not consent. This supersedes the earlier 24-profile summary without asserting full usable audience coverage.

The revenue readers and bridge have local tests plus the earlier approved one-account provider test read; they do not establish complete capture populations, allocations, bank settlement or recurring revenue. No operational AC audit collector, approved Industry research source, model-enabled agent, Runtime Production schedule or fresh same-cycle SEO/AEO parity is verified. Manual AC reads and inactive 527/528 routing are dated evidence, not an operating journey. Source health still lacks its continuous window. September 4 Adzuna counts (97 fresh canonical and 1,108 stale lowercase rows) are historical; merged #351 does not prove current freshness or cleanup. Outcome, measurement and learning tables still lack accepted operational outcomes.

Contact form-to-inbox routing was accepted for the one approved September 8 synthetic submission. It is separate from Gmail opportunity intake; durable notification-result tracking, automatic recovery and retention remain absent.

### What still requires Autumn

The completed #357 owner login/synthetic acceptance, the one-account provider test, contact migration/activation/test and merged releases #350–#357/#359/#360 require no repeat approval. Earlier pending-review, missing-table and disabled-contact statements remain dated history. Current code includes #351 Adzuna safeguards; fresh collector health and any legacy-row cleanup/deactivation remain separate. No completed release or test grants Runtime Production authority.

Remaining consequential decisions concern exact additional source/read and historical-row inspection/repair scopes; hosted role/provider acceptance using suitable accounts; AC evidence population, dedicated opportunity consent configuration, journey activation and observed parity; concrete Draft/notification/send actions; fresh search parity and safe publication; supported Elite/team outcomes before new Agency purchase; and future Runtime Production, models, schedules, execution and new database writes. The local first-value and saved-profile rules already exist; further milestone definitions must reuse acquisition-owned evidence. Credential rotation remains unauthorized and the hiring-firm product remains deferred. The roster assigns preparation work but grants no new live-action authority.

9. `SEO-004`, `WORKFLOW-007`, `WORKFLOW-008`, `SEC-001`, `SEC-002` — scope fresh search parity and remaining credential-class review while preserving current controls. No retirement, rotation, publication or schedule activation is authorized here.
10. `ELITE-001`, `AGENCY-001`, `TRACE-001` — define supportable service/team outcomes and an evidence-backed outcome loop. New Agency purchase and the hiring-firm deferral remain unchanged; row counts do not establish completion percentage.

### Audit limitations and evidence rule

- Repository, GitHub Issue/PR, Vercel artifact, tracked configuration, migrations, scripts, tests, docs, adapters, and the isolated/shared working trees were inspected.
- This pass did not query live Outseta records, ActiveCampaign assets, Supabase row contents, GA4, Search Console, PageSpeed, Adzuna, Gmail, current cron executions, or payment settlement. Read-only, header-only Supabase requests verified that the configured project exposes the expected tracker and AI-usage tables/columns; they did not inspect records or prove writes. All other facts remain unknown unless an earlier dated, environment-specific acceptance record is cited.
- No production build was run. No database write, deployment, promotion, merge, email, model call, schedule change, external mutation, issue edit, or PR edit occurred during this repair. Read-only GitHub/commit-status and Supabase schema checks were performed.
- A filename, route, type, registration, interface, test name, PR title, merge, READY deployment, or environment-variable name is never enough by itself to claim operational completion.

## Status semantics

Only these values are allowed in the canonical matrix:

| Status | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `planned` | Requirement is defined; a working implementation is not yet evidenced. |
| `in_progress` | Some real implementation or reconciliation exists, but material required behavior remains. |
| `implemented_unverified` | Working code appears present, but the relevant execution or environment has not been validated. |
| `validated_local` | Current-source tests or a local fixture execution passed. |
| `validated_staging` | The relevant staging schema or behavior was actually validated in staging. |
| `verified_preview` | The behavior was actually exercised on an identified Vercel Preview. |
| `production_live` | The behavior was verified in the existing Production environment with explicit evidence. |
| `blocked` | Progress requires a missing dependency, authority, source, or decision. |
| `deferred` | Autumn intentionally postponed the work. |
| `superseded` | A later decision or implementation replaced this item; history remains. |

Composite rows take the lowest defensible status for their full requirement. A lower status does not erase a deployed sub-capability; the evidence column records that nuance.

## Agent maturity inventory

Maturity is the highest rung actually evidenced, not the intended destination:

1. `contract_only` — types, registration, interface, or placeholder only.
2. `deterministic_v1` — real business logic runs against fixtures without a model.
3. `model_capable` — a guarded model adapter is attached to the agent, though it may be disabled.
4. `sensor_connected` — the agent consumes a real or approved read-only source.
5. `persistence_connected` — runs, signals, tasks, and outputs persist durably.
6. `preview_verified` — the agent path passed a Vercel Preview or staging deployment test.
7. `production_enabled` — Autumn explicitly approved it and it is operating in Production.

The generic OpenAI adapter does not raise an agent's maturity by itself. No model call was made during acceptance, and no specialist is classified as `model_capable` solely because the SDK or adapter exists.

## Workflow maturity inventory

2026-09-08 local acceptance addendum (historical; superseded as current maturity by the September 9 hosted evidence below): `specialist-review-v1` now exercises Revenue, Growth, Industry and Marketing through the real protected fixture factory and Workflow test adapter, with nonempty outputs and in-memory artifact persistence. The four specialists remain `deterministic_v1`, not `persistence_connected` or `preview_verified`; no new Supabase row or hosted workflow was created. Baseline, distinct-review isolation and duplicate reuse passed. This adds substantive local plumbing evidence, not live business facts or model capability.

2026-09-09 hosted acceptance addendum (current): the approved owner Preview at `6644f31200d6d593f9f7102e58d052529e28090c` completed quiet baseline, substantive specialist-review-v1 and fresh-form repeat. Four specialists now have nonempty durable Supabase staging evidence and `preview_verified` maturity; this supersedes the September 8 addendum only as current status, not as historical test evidence. Provider completion and unchanged exact business IDs/counts/digests establish fresh-delivery duplicate protection. Inputs remain invented: no real-source connection, model, email, external action, schedule or Production enablement. Exact signed-nonce replay and live non-owner denial were not exercised.

“Persists to staging” distinguishes a verified staging row from code that merely contains a store. “Scheduled” distinguishes a repository cron declaration from a verified active schedule.

### Required environment-variable names

Values are intentionally excluded. Runtime base and safety: `AGENT_RUNTIME_ENV`, `AGENT_RUNTIME_MODE`, `AGENT_MUTATIONS_ENABLED`, `AGENT_MODEL_EXECUTION_ENABLED`, `AGENT_WORKFLOW_PROVIDER`, `AGENT_RUNTIME_VERSION`, `AGENT_TRACE_NAMESPACE`, and platform `VERCEL_ENV`. Preview: `AGENT_PREVIEW_API_TOKEN`, `AGENT_PREVIEW_SYNTHETIC_ONLY`, `AGENT_PREVIEW_PERSISTENCE_ENABLED`. Durable staging: `AGENT_DURABLE_PERSISTENCE_ENABLED`, `AGENT_DURABLE_SYNTHETIC_ONLY`, `AGENT_STAGING_WORKFLOW_TOKEN`, `AGENT_STAGING_PROJECT_REF`, `SUPABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`. Protected Runtime admin: `AGENT_ADMIN_ENABLED`, `AGENT_ADMIN_SHARED_SECRET`, `AGENT_ADMIN_AUTUMN_SUBJECT_ID`, `AGENT_ADMIN_ALLOWED_ORIGIN`. Optional model configuration: `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_AGENT_MODEL`, `OPENAI_AGENT_MAX_TURNS`.

Members transport: `INTELLIGENCE_OS_ADMIN_ENABLED`, `INTELLIGENCE_OS_ADMIN_SHARED_SECRET`, `INTELLIGENCE_OS_AUTUMN_SUBJECT_ID`, `INTELLIGENCE_OS_ADMIN_ALLOWED_ORIGIN`, `INTELLIGENCE_OS_AGENT_RUNTIME_URL`, and optional `INTELLIGENCE_OS_AGENT_RUNTIME_BYPASS_SECRET`. Source-specific names belong in their owned task rows and must never include values.

### Architecture decisions

- 2026-08-24 — Reuse PR #317's `conversion_events` as the sole raw first-party behavior/conversion ledger; do not build a second conversion store.
- 2026-08-26 — Treat Outseta as membership authority and Supabase as an auditable canonical projection, preserving unknowns and conflicts.
- 2026-08-31 — Use durable Vercel Workflow plus destination-bound Supabase staging; keep deterministic agents viable without model credentials.
- 2026-09-01 — Require signed service requests, stable owner subject, one-use database nonces, immutable approved payload/version, and no executor by default.
- 2026-09-03 — Treat code, deployment, configuration, connection, and operation as separate facts; only environment-specific evidence advances status.
- 2026-09-03 — Keep extension registrations at `contract_only` until real logic proves a higher maturity rung.
- 2026-09-03 — Require the canonical ledger update in the same PR as any governed code change.
- 2026-09-03 — For payment reconciliation, preserve both authorities rather than flattening them: Stripe owns money movement and Outseta owns membership/access state; a conflict is an actionable signal, not an inferred resolution.
- 2026-09-03 — Member-tool access is default-deny by individual route and capability. A local browser-only calculator may be enabled after authentication, entitlement, conservative-assumption, and deterministic-test review without enabling remote AI, weather, tracker, or persistence APIs.
- 2026-09-03 — The expanded tool repair uses one default-deny capability matrix across catalog presentation, middleware routing, server page authorization, Jobs actions, and connected APIs. A visible Open button is never authorization; protected pages and APIs independently authenticate the Outseta session and enforce plan access before database or provider work.
- 2026-09-03 — Use `plan-config.ts` as the single repository source for recognized Outseta plan UIDs and display names. Profile presentation and server-side conversion naming must consume that source; a missing or unknown live claim is displayed as unavailable rather than being replaced by a potentially stale Supabase tier projection.
- 2026-09-04 — The scheduled `sync-adzuna` route is the current canonical collector. It must require the configured bearer secret, treat any source/HTTP/payload/empty-set failure as an unsuccessful run, upsert a complete replacement set before stale-row cleanup, and preserve existing listings when confidence is incomplete. The unscheduled `/api/jobs/refresh` path and lowercase legacy rows remain separately review-gated rather than silently deleted.

- 2026-09-02 — Inspector profiles are private: visitors cannot browse them and paid members can see only their own profile.
- 2026-09-03 — Direct Free signup and onboarding should lead to first value before a behavioral upgrade prompt.
- 2026-09-03 — Agency visibility and Elite human-service promises require an explicit supportability decision while the firm lane is deferred.
- 2026-09-03 — The Agency plan remains in the member product lane and must be described as an inspector-team plan; firm-product work remains deferred independently.
- 2026-09-03 — Signed-in Free, Starter, Founders, Pro, Elite, and Agency members may use the income scenario planner. Elite and Agency may additionally use route economics. Visitors see the catalog but must sign in to run either calculator.
- 2026-09-03 — Agency remains visible as the member-side inspector-team plan, but the local repair disables new purchase until seats, roles, administration, and fulfillment are verified. Existing Agency members retain the reviewed calculator access. Reopening purchase requires Autumn's product approval.
- 2026-09-03 — Current tool access is: Free = Income Scenarios only; Pro = Income, client/vendor tracking, company tracking, AI Concierge, AI Resume, job tracking, weather, and route planning; Elite and existing Agency = all nine tools including Route Economics. Legacy Starter/Founders retain client/company/AI/job tracking plus Income. This access decision does not claim that every connected provider is healthy or reopen Agency purchase.

#### DATA-008 reviewed application scope

- Repository change: `supabase/migrations/20260904183000_create_contact_submissions_server_only.sql`, its executable PostgreSQL tests, one pinned test-only dependency, the existing member CI check, and this owned ledger row. No member UI or entitlement behavior changes.
- Approval target: this one forward migration against the explicitly verified member-site Supabase destination. Do not run the entire migration backlog. First recheck relation shape, referenced UUID columns, role availability, and existing policies without selecting contact contents.
- Existing-row handling: no historical message is rewritten, truncated, or deleted. Null timestamps, invalid storage bounds, or unrecognized policies abort the transaction for private review. The migration has a five-second lock wait and a thirty-second statement limit.
- Verification after an approved application: read schema and grants, then separately authorize a synthetic receipt transaction with rollback to verify service-role access and browser denial. This database check does not send a form, invoke a webhook, or prove email delivery. A later end-to-end form/notification test needs its own explicit scope.
- Recovery: failure rolls back the transaction; after success do not drop a table that may contain newly received messages. Preserve server-only grants and use a reviewed forward correction if needed. No automatic destructive down migration is supplied.
- Still withheld: remote database application, live receipt writes, notifications, merge, Production rollout, and any claim of live contact-form recovery. Existing audit facts remain dated to their original verification; this increment adds repository and local-test evidence only.
- 2026-09-06 approval/execution update: the previously withheld exact migration and rollback receipt test were explicitly approved and succeeded. All other exclusions above remain. Live checks proved server insert/read/update and eight browser denials, followed by independent confirmation that the synthetic receipt was absent. #### DATA-008 direct contact-routing replacement (2026-09-06 ET)

- Ownership and checkpoint: `DATA-008` only; branch `codex/318-contact-direct-email`, HEAD/base `741683c448116f3b4571050af84cee9803c09af4`. The pending form-delivery ledger audit was carried intact when creating this branch. Unrelated shared-checkout work, ActiveCampaign-owned rows and code, provider settings, and the existing receipt table were preserved. The prior audit above remains a dated account of the pre-replacement code.
- Current local behavior: `/api/contact` validates one reply mailbox, preserves existing rate limiting and service-only receipt storage, generates the stored receipt ID on the server, and then awaits a dedicated Google notification attempt. No contact data is sent to n8n and there is no legacy fallback. The internal message uses the fixed public info sender and recipient; Reply-To contains the validated visitor mailbox. Receipt identity appears only in the email, not operational logs. Plain-text MIME and encoded subject handling prevent user content from becoming headers or HTML.
- User-visible behavior: a successful HTTP response alone is no longer a receipt. The component requires explicit saved state, captures the form before awaiting network completion, and separates saved messages from notification acceptance. A failed/unconfigured notification offers a direct info-mailbox follow-up without inviting duplicate form submission. Failed or uncertain storage responses retain entered values.
- Sending controls: the sender is server-only and does nothing unless `CONTACT_EMAIL_ENABLED` is exactly `true`, `VERCEL_ENV` is exactly `production`, and all three dedicated variables `CONTACT_GMAIL_CLIENT_ID`, `CONTACT_GMAIL_CLIENT_SECRET`, and `CONTACT_GMAIL_REFRESH_TOKEN` are nonempty. Local and Vercel Preview never send, even with copied credentials or enable flags. No variable or secret value was read, installed, or changed by this increment.
- Transport safeguards: fixed Google OAuth and Gmail REST endpoints, rejected redirects, no cache, one five-second deadline covering both requests and response reads, required successful token/message response shapes, and fixed redacted failure labels. There are no automatic retries because a send timeout can happen after provider acceptance. No user acknowledgement email or historical-message replay is implemented; the only proposed send is the internal contact alert. Provider acceptance remains different from verified inbox delivery.
- Connection and activation still required: confirm Google Workspace as the provider; use a dedicated OAuth client with Gmail API and the minimum `gmail.send` scope, consented offline by the authorized mailbox owner. Confirm the public info identity is that mailbox or an approved send-as identity. Store its three server-only credentials in the member project's Production scope without placing them in chat, this ledger, source control, or browser bundles. Leave the enable flag off until the exact release and synthetic test are approved. Existing analytics OAuth credentials and the assistant's connected Gmail permissions must not be reused.
- Bounded future acceptance: after explicit connection/configuration and release approval, submit one clearly synthetic contact through the deployed form, verify one durable receipt, one notification in the info inbox, and the Reply action's visitor destination. Any actual reply send or cleanup of that test receipt needs an explicit scope. Check failed-notification handling locally; do not intentionally send duplicates to test retries. No live acceptance has occurred.
- Recovery: disable the dedicated contact-mail flag to stop Google requests while continuing to save receipts. Preserve the table and incoming messages. Do not restore the retired n8n URL or replay saved requests automatically. Notification-result persistence, a retry queue, recipient-delivery telemetry, and retention are still separate follow-ups, not hidden promises of this patch.
- Repository evidence: `apps/web-members/lib/contact-email-message.ts`, `lib/contact-email-sender.ts`, `app/api/contact/route.ts`, `app/contact-us/ContactForm.tsx`, and the five `tests/contact-*.test.mjs` files. `.github/workflows/member-public-surfaces.yml` runs the focused tests, lint, and typecheck without live mail credentials.
- Local verification: 45 test cases passed with zero skips: 13 executable isolated PostgreSQL migration/permission fixtures, 11 real-route fixtures with mocked storage and provider calls, four message/routing cases, nine sender groups, and eight actual React-element submit-handler cases. Full member lint, TypeScript, and member-surface audit passed. Independent review found and corrected lost field/rate-limit guidance; explicit rejection messages and matching field limits are now tested. The initial route test harness expected the old error-log channel; it was corrected to assert the sender's fixed redacted warning labels and rerun successfully. No browser form-to-inbox acceptance, Google credential validation, Preview deployment, or Production email occurred; no production build was run.

These document the API contract; they do not prove this mailbox is connected.

- Applied the five merged migrations (`20260827090000`, `20260827110000`, `20260827120000`, `20260827130000`, `20260827140000`) through the verified staging SQL editor. C3, C5, C6 and C7 rollback-safe validations passed.
- C8 validation initially failed with SQLSTATE `42883`: the restricted `trace_agent_action_decision()` search path could not resolve `digest`, because staging installs pgcrypto in `extensions`. The C8 schema had committed; its failed synthetic validation left no member/action rows. No validation failure was reported as a pass.
- Applied the focused follow-up `20260902023000_fix_agent_decision_trace_hash.sql`, using schema-qualified PostgreSQL built-in SHA-256 without widening the function search path or granting privileges. C8 then passed, including a new checksum assertion; C7 approval/rejection/replay validation also passed again with the C8 trigger active. Final synthetic action and trace counts were both zero.
- Reran C3/C5/C6 validations against the final repaired C8 schema: all passed. Post-rollback counts for members, runs, actions, sensor observations, owners, and destination bindings were all zero.
- Local acceptance: 64 Node tests, 7 real Workflow fixture tests, both TypeScript configurations, formatting, all migration/static checks, Preview safety checks, specialist checks, and `git diff --check` passed. Workflow tests initially failed under the Windows filesystem sandbox, then passed outside it with no dependency changes. No Next.js production build was run.
- Owner identity: a single read-only exact-account Outseta lookup resolved a stable subject; the user explicitly confirmed it. The raw account address and subject are redacted from this canonical ledger. No Outseta mutation occurred.
- Applied `supabase/operations/20260902_activate_issue318_staging_owner.sql` only to the verified staging project. It refuses nonempty registries, inserts the sole owner and active destination binding, and verifies both service-role authorization functions before committing. The final binding readback confirmed the correct staging reference and fingerprint.
- Vercel configuration is restricted to branch `codex/318-staging-acceptance`: Runtime durable persistence and owner controls are enabled in dry-run/synthetic mode; Members uses the staging database rather than inherited Preview credentials. Model execution and external mutations remain false. Protected server transport preserves HMAC, uses a server-only platform bypass header, and rejects redirects.
- Credential incident: browser diagnostic output exposed the previous staging database secret and Runtime automation bypass. Replacements were created and the old credentials revoked. No Production credential was involved; no credential values are retained in repository evidence. Diagnostic snapshots are now redacted before output.
- Members acceptance mode suppresses GTM/ActiveCampaign tracking, server marketing events/tags, and conversion collection before rate-limiter/database access. Five focused mocked transport/isolation tests, the protected-admin audit, TypeScript, and targeted lint passed; no Next.js production build was run.
- Remaining: finish branch-config readback, deploy the updated Previews, complete the authenticated durable/protected smoke, and run the report-parity cycle. These are pending, not passed. Agent Runtime Production activation, real-member exports, models, email, external executors, and schedules remain disabled.
- PR #324 is still open and superseded by the merged correction; do not merge it. No PR was merged or closed during this checkpoint.

References: [Outseta stable user identity](https://go.outseta.com/support/kb/articles/XQYMXqQP/the-jwt-access-token), [PostgreSQL built-in SHA-256](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/functions-binarystring.html).

## Safety invariants

- Do not merge pull requests, deploy or promote Production, change the Agent Runtime Production Branch to `main`, apply a production migration, add Production variables, or attach a production/custom domain.
- Do not connect the Agent Runtime to production Supabase or copy production members/contacts into staging.
- Do not send email, publish content, mutate ActiveCampaign, change pricing/subscriptions/entitlements, perform bulk cleanup, or attach an external mutation executor.
- Model execution remains disabled by default. Tests must pass without an OpenAI key.
- Persist operational inputs, structured outputs, evidence, concise rationale, decisions, tool metadata, status, errors, usage, outcomes, and learning only. Never persist private chain-of-thought.
- Existing collectors and scheduled reports remain until a successful parity cycle proves the replacement path.

## Cross-increment architecture findings

- C3 should begin with one synthetic-only durable workflow, `lifecycle-integrity-check@c3-v1`, and use Workflow's managed durability rather than adding Vercel Queues without a real fan-out requirement.
- C3 must use atomic business-idempotency claims, first-writer Workflow binding, bounded transactional persistence, readback verification, dispatch leases, and at-least-once semantics. Do not claim exactly-once execution.
- A committed allowlist plus a service-role-only database sentinel must bind Preview to the reviewed staging destination. Environment variables alone are not sufficient proof, and the known production project must always be rejected.
- C5 must implement the exact stable workflow names `conversion_review`, `daily_business_health`, and `weekly_operating_review`; healthy daily fixtures must stay quiet and weekly review must emit at most three priorities.
- C6 must preserve existing SEO/AEO/content report shapes for one parity cycle while distinguishing fixture/baseline/live observations and recording source health, staleness, checksums, correlation, and causation.
- C6 ActiveCampaign access must be bounded, paginated, allowlisted, GET-only, registry-approved, and keyed by stable contact IDs with collision handling. Existing mutation modules remain outside agent tool exposure.
- C7 approval must use a distinct stable Autumn subject, bounded bodies, same-origin/CSRF checks, nonces or decision idempotency keys, payload digests, compare-and-set status/version updates, and durable events. Approval never executes the action.
- C7 triggers must not reuse query-string secrets or treat `x-vercel-cron: 1` as authentication. No Production schedule is activated in this program.

Autumn separately approved definition metadata for automations **473, 474, 475, 477, 478, 479, 480, 484, 513, 518**, and up to **24 recent Nested Objects profiles**, reconciling only unique stored Outseta person/account and AC contact IDs. Raw member records were transient, not written to files or repository artifacts. No message bodies or unrelated-asset contents were analyzed.

### Bounded member comparison: observed facts and limits

The selector was the 24 most recently updated profiles, with deterministic ID ordering. Each stored AC contact ID and Outseta person ID was reverse-checked for uniqueness across profiles; the exact Outseta person/account relation and exact AC contact record were read back. All 24 links passed; no email match established authority. Ecommerce back-links were unavailable as corroboration and did not veto or establish membership. This is a convenience sample, not representative cohort statistics.

- Outseta plan IDs mapped to 8 Free, 2 Pro, 3 Elite and 11 legacy Founders. Stages were 12 Trialing (2), 10 Subscribing (3), and 2 Past due (7). **Free is included in Outseta stage 2**, so raw stage is not sufficient to infer a paid trial or upgrade eligibility. No Agency/Starter/churned/internal/coworker/test/demo/hiring-firm population count was established; those cases are locally fixture-tested only.
- Six stored projection statuses differed from the documented numeric billing-stage mapping: four stage-2 Founders and two stage-3 paid records. This establishes projection disagreement, not a Stripe settlement or access conclusion.
- Eight contacts had multiple observed plan tags. Both stage-7 Elite records carried **active, canceled and past_due** tags. Two stage-3 paid records carried canceled tags. These positive conflicts can expose current/past-due members to incompatible journeys if conditions use the stale labels; actual enrollment or wrong delivery was not read or proven.
- The expected plan tag was observed for 23 records; the remaining record's expected tag was **not observed**, not proven absent. Eleven retained Wix markers despite current authoritative member links. All 24 had an observed active list-12 relationship; no affirmative consent conclusion follows.
- Contact tag/list relationship endpoints returned arrays without `meta.total`; a bounded shape check returned the same row count at offsets 0 and 100. Consequently no complete paginated relationship coverage is claimed. Positive conflicting labels are valid observations, while absent tags, absent subscriptions and all-contact consent coverage remain unknown. The August 31 audit and failed historical recurringPayments query are leads only, not current missing-record findings.

### Synchronization trace and first local correction

Person signup/update and account subscription events enter the signed Outseta webhook, map a profile, select/update the projection by email, then invoke deep-data synchronization and paid-lifecycle event helpers. The exact existing exception includes AC contact upsert, list-12 subscription, plan/status/persona/source/launch tags, ecommerce customer/order/recurring mirrors and renewal field 187, and server-side events. This task did not run that path live, freeze it, or add a new write path.

| Event or failure | Verified code behavior and risk | Local disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Signup / full subscription | Before this patch numeric stage 3 fell through to canceled; stage 7 fell through to active; stage 6 mapped to past_due; substring matching could call Trial Expired a trial | Dedicated documented mapping now handles stages 2–8, exact labels, disagreement and unknowns; exercised through the real route with mocked dependencies |
| Plan-light Person update | Paid tier/plan preservation existed, but absent stage defaulted to active and could replace a past-due/canceled/trial status | Missing/unknown/conflicting billing evidence becomes null; existing database status is not overwritten; unknown lifecycle does not create an ACTIVE recurring mirror or replace status tags |
| Trial / pending cancellation | Existing projection compresses stage 4 Canceling into canceled and stage 8 Cancelling trial into trialing; Free is also stage 2 | Compression retained to avoid an unreviewed schema/access change. Coordinated lifecycle adapter must use raw Outseta evidence and preserve pending cancellation/Free distinctions before live journey eligibility |
| Upgrade / downgrade | Plan UID/name feeds plan tags and nominal ecommerce mirrors; existing paid-plan-change helper includes downgrades and can emit purchase-style intent events | Traced only; acquisition-owned conversion instrumentation unchanged. Do not report nominal orders/events as collected revenue |
| Cancellation / failed payment | Wrong mapping and existing accumulated labels can produce active/canceled/past_due overlap | Mapping corrected locally; historical labels are not backfilled automatically |
| Duplicate / out-of-order delivery | Code comment says equal timestamps skip, but comparison is strictly greater; account events use nested person's timestamp; concurrent/equal deliveries can repeat side effects | Confirmed code risk; durable identity/event receipts and scoped retry recovery remain next work |
| Partial failure / retry | Database write precedes AC calls; AC failures are caught while webhook can return 200. A later stale-event skip can prevent recovery | No recovery guarantee claimed; require per-step status and bounded retry proposal without duplicating conversion storage |
| Tag cleanup | Cleanup relies on included tag metadata and only its returned relationships, removes old associations before additions, and treats 422 broadly | Coverage/partial-removal risk remains; exact current dependency graph required before changing associations |
| Consent / list overlap | Legacy sync unconditionally requests list 12 status 1 and ecommerce acceptsMarketing 1 | Confirmed resubscription risk; no observed unauthorized send asserted. A separate consent-preserving correction must be prepared; current exception is not widened |

The billing mapping follows [Outseta account billing stages](https://go.outseta.com/support/kb/articles/Kj9boWnd/account-billing-stages). ActiveCampaign confirms that changing an unsubscribed list relationship to active re-subscribes it in its [list status API documentation](https://developers.activecampaign.com/reference/update-list-status-for-contact). No credential-bearing scripts were published.

### Proposed journey specification and measurable onboarding increment

`evaluateMarketingJourneys` is a pure local evaluator, surfaced as reviewable decisions and a synthetic interactive simulator. All decisions contain `mutationAllowed=false`. It does not enroll, subscribe, send, persist approvals, or grant access. Source, tier, lifecycle, engagement and consent remain independent. Phase C now requires a separate uniquely matched, verified Outseta truth input for marketing classification; a Supabase profile carrying Outseta IDs can no longer manufacture that authority. The existing projection store and conversion instrumentation are unchanged; absent/duplicate/unverified truth is withheld. Unknown/conflicting authority and ambiguous identities are withheld; internal/coworker/test/demo/hiring-firm traits remain excluded even on a paid account. Agency retains member-side status.

Common promotional exclusions: member age under 30 days or unknown; incomplete/active/unknown onboarding; missing approved first-value evidence; missing profile/geography/experience/inspection-type inputs; trial/past-due/paused/canceling/conflicting/unknown lifecycle; paid tenure under 30 days or unknown; denied/unknown marketing consent including unsubscribe/bounce; another active journey; incomplete enrollment history; missing lifecycle-cycle key. Proposed frequency is at most one promotional contact in seven days. A stable member + journey + lifecycle-cycle key prevents repeat enrollment within that cycle. One highest-priority journey wins. These are local proposal rules; no existing automation was changed.

Service rules are separate: necessary onboarding/payment help requires an independently authorized delivery channel and complete routing/history evidence, with a proposed daily limit. Marketing opt-out is never permission to resubscribe; a hard-bounced email address is not an authorized service email channel. Existing transactional or in-app delivery must be verified by the journey owner. All journeys stop on changed authority, conflicting identity, relevant delivery exclusion or loss of eligibility; promotions stop immediately on trial/paid conversion, onboarding restart or consent withdrawal.

| Priority / goal | Trigger and audience | Proposed steps / specific exits | Frequency / success metric / dependencies |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 Payment help: resolve a real failed-payment state | Fresh Outseta past_due and uniquely linked member | Explain account action and link to existing billing self-service; exit when authoritative past_due ends | Once per cycle, daily service cap; Outseta recovery and separately verified Stripe settlement; authorized service channel and fresh billing evidence |
| 2 Activation onboarding: achieve first value | Active/trialing member with known not-started/active onboarding | Existing start guide → complete profile/geography/experience/type → Income Scenarios first useful result; exit completion or lifecycle change | Once per cycle, daily service cap; seven-day first-value rate; JOURNEY-001/002/003 and approved milestone |
| 3 Cancellation feedback: learn a useful reason | Fresh canceling/canceled lifecycle, authorized channel | One neutral optional feedback request, then acknowledgment only if appropriate; exit response or membership recovery | One request per lifecycle cycle; response rate and categorized reasons; existing receipt/channel and privacy contract |
| 4 Trial offer: test an expressed need | Active Free, all promotional guards, explicit trial need and confirmed trial eligibility | Explain approved trial scope → existing plan flow; exit trial start, upgrade, decline or exclusion | Seven-day cap, once per cycle; trial first-value then settled conversion; approved terms, DATA-004 and acquisition evidence |
| 5 Free → Pro: solve an established tool need | Active Free, all guards, expressed Pro need | One relevant established Pro capability → current plan comparison; exit upgrade/trial/decline/exclusion | Seven-day cap, once per cycle; verified activation and settled conversion; current Pro fulfillment and approved offer |
| 6 Free → Elite: solve a need for the current full tool set | Active Free, all guards, expressed Elite need | Relevant current Elite tool outcome → current plan comparison; exit upgrade/trial/decline/exclusion | Seven-day cap, once per cycle; first value and settled conversion; no invented concierge capacity, response time or work guarantee |
| 7 Re-engagement: resume useful member activity | Active member, at least 90 days of evidenced meaningful inactivity, all promotional guards | One relevant help prompt → a currently entitled action; exit meaningful activity or exclusion | Seven-day cap, once per cycle; useful action within seven days; complete action coverage, not email-open inactivity |
| 8 Win-back: return an ended membership to value | Canceled/inactive, all promotional guards, approved offer; eligible cancellation feedback takes priority | Acknowledge prior context → relevant current value → existing signup flow; exit return, decline or exclusion | Seven-day cap, once per cycle; authoritative return and separately settled payment; cancellation timing/offer approval and fresh lifecycle |

First measurable proposal for JOURNEY owners: use the existing onboarding guide to lead a new Free member to one useful Income Scenarios result. Proposed first-value evidence is a successful calculation with required inputs, recorded once for the stable member; **a page visit, profile save or email open is not this milestone**. No accepted event currently proves it, so promotions fail closed. Autumn and inspector acquisition must approve the milestone and reuse/extend their existing evidence architecture; this task must not add another conversion store or independently instrument it. Draft onboarding copy for review: “Start with an income scenario. Complete your profile, then use Income Scenarios to explore how your inspection work could fit your goals. Your Free plan also includes a limited three-firm directory preview.” No unrestricted Free directory or paid-tool promise.

Measurement boundary: reuse authoritative signup, approved first-value evidence, existing acquisition-owned `conversion_events`, and Stripe settlement evidence. No scoring engine or dashboard is included in this sprint. Speculative score weights and a separate baseline waiting period are withdrawn; they are not prerequisites for shipping the foundational fixes. No conversion lift is yet measured.

### First proposed live correction and recovery boundary

The first release candidate changes only future existing webhook processing in `app/api/webhooks/outseta/route.ts`, `lib/outseta-billing-stage.ts`, and the unknown-recurring-state guard in `lib/active-campaign-deep-data.ts`. It corrects numeric status mapping and declines to invent ACTIVE when evidence is absent/conflicting. Exact effects are the already-existing profile status projection, status tag and recurring mirror driven by a subsequently delivered event. No bulk historical repair, new endpoint, enrollment, campaign, notification, send, schedule or authority change is included.

Before a Production approval request is ready: verify null status on new inserts against the actual approved database schema/Preview path; exercise signup, stage 3/6/7, partial Person events, cancellation compression and Free-stage semantics; inspect status/field triggers in the approved ten automations; review effects on existing event consumers with inspector acquisition. Then Autumn must approve the exact release/merge/Production rollout. Recovery is reverting that code release and verifying future event handling; reverting code does not undo emitted tags or mail. Any association rollback needs its own exact before-state and trigger-reviewed approval. This candidate is locally reviewable, **not cleared for Production**.

The first subsequent cleanup candidate is the observed conflicting-plan/status subset within the approved 24, revalidated at execution time using stable IDs. Private exact association IDs, expected additions/removals, before-state, trigger effects and rollback must be prepared after dependency reads. The public ledger intentionally contains only aggregates; this is not an authorized tag batch. Existing list subscriptions and automation states are unchanged.

Parity acceptance for AC-008: after an approved replacement exists, observe one full lifecycle cycle across old/replacement decisions using the same bounded cohort and time window; account for entries, exclusions, authorized necessary messages, exits, duplicates, errors and first-value/payment outcomes. A zero unexplained targeting-conflict result and successful recovery checks must be recorded before presenting exact retire IDs. Simulation, fixtures and a partial week do not satisfy observed parity. Keep all old automations until Autumn's separate retirement approval.

Opportunity email retains four distinct gates: approved unscheduled AC Draft creation, approved notification to Autumn, recorded owner approval/rejection, and separate send authorization. No Draft, notification, approval record or send was created in this increment. The owner-only admin/approval implementation remains with the Intelligence OS owner; proposed Astra tools should consume that boundary, not create another decision store.

### Local correction and its limits

`apps/web-members/lib/active-campaign-deep-data.ts`, `lib/active-campaign-sync-result.ts` and the existing Outseta route now implement:

- No mailing-list status writes from membership events. Existing active, unconfirmed, unsubscribed, bounced and unknown choices remain unchanged. New contacts are not automatically subscribed; an independently approved opt-in flow is required for marketing. Existing in-app onboarding remains available. This narrows the existing exception without freezing contact/tag/ecommerce synchronization or adding a new write path.
- Existing ecommerce customers are read/reused by stored ID or unique external-person/connection IDs, preserving their marketing flag. New mirrors use `acceptsMarketing=0`; membership does not assert opt-in. A stale email association is withheld as a transport-link conflict, not repaired through an email-only authority join. No existing customer flag is overwritten.
- Explicit per-step success, submitted, failed, blocked and skipped results; failed HTTP/schema/transport/GraphQL responses and failed Supabase link writes can no longer produce an unconditional “sync complete.” Diagnostics omit member identifiers, emails, raw provider errors and payloads. Requests reject redirects and time out after ten seconds each.
- Independent tag sync continues after a customer failure; customer-dependent mirrors stop. A replacement tag must be positively acknowledged or observed before its old conflicting association is removed. Already-observed associations are not re-added. A 400/409/422 response is not assumed to mean “already exists”; exact identity readback is required. Failed tag/field reads cannot authorize blind creation or deletion.
- The webhook reports `profileSynced=true` separately from AC failure, with `recoveryRequired=true` and `automaticRetry=false` when appropriate. HTTP 200 still acknowledges the committed membership projection; it does **not** establish marketing completion. Recurring-payment responses are recorded as submission receipts, not completed settlement or completed asynchronous processing.

Meaningful remaining limits: no durable per-step recovery receipt, queue, automatic replay or whole-event duplicate guarantee was added. Equal/concurrent deliveries can still repeat order/recurring/event side effects; older-event skips can still bypass recovery. The route's email-based projection lookup and raw account/person event ordering remain DATA-004/AC-007 work. Existing acquisition-owned conversion/event calls were not redesigned. Ten-second per-request bounds are not a whole-webhook execution guarantee. Contact relationship pagination still lacks verified totals, so positive observations support repairs but absent relationships are not declared missing.

Release effects require review: stopping forced opt-in can reduce delivery from list-dependent marketing automations for new contacts; this is not permission to bypass consent with service-email claims. Adding a replacement tag before removal can briefly expose both tags to existing automation conditions. A historical cleanup must therefore review the exact condition dependencies and current before-state; a code revert cannot undo an email already triggered.

### 2026-09-08 — OS-005 strict first-value evidence adapter, local only

The ActiveCampaign owner delegated only a pure conversion of acquisition-owned stored completion rows into the existing `JourneyMilestoneEvidence`. OS-005 owns this adapter; AC-004, the acquisition producer and their append histories are preserved. Isolated branch `codex/318-first-value-evidence` began clean at `4881a51851da50fffc802c90ee16db0de6f45a6d`. After the producer was published, a verified non-overlapping fast-forward incorporated main `cec214133e6bcdbcf7dad24b14fae137abd8f60b` without stashing, discarding or overwriting the three adapter files. Implementation commit is `05acd77c48eecaf2f51feb532b7a5ae23e779ed6`. The shared dirty checkout and other local branches were not changed.

`adaptIncomeScenarioMilestone` accepts three supplied, complete, bounded snapshots for one requested Outseta person and current subscription: canonical profiles, independently verified current Outseta membership truth, and stored completion receipts. Each must resolve to exactly one row and satisfy a caller-specified freshness budget; partial, missing, stale, future or ambiguous observations are withheld. The adapter performs no fetch or persistence. It cannot establish provenance from an arbitrary object: future approved read wiring must supply the stated source coverage and authority. There is deliberately no default live freshness policy.

The adapter requires a unique exact `profiles.outseta_person_uid` match, current verified authoritative Outseta subscription UID, real receipt UUID and server `occurred_at`, exact event/page/source/version metadata, and the producer's deterministic per-person/per-subscription SHA-256 key. No email, account, anonymous, generic profile-completion or old-tag fallback exists. Wrong-event rows that previously occupied the reserved key are withheld; the adapter neither repairs them nor treats browser `recorded:true` as a database row. | First | Configure account-wide Active Intelligence instructions and the existing Brand Kit with inspector-first positioning, actual Free/Pro/Elite/Agency/legacy limits, private profiles, source/consent separation, 30-day-and-completion rule, and approval-only changes | Inspect and merge with existing settings before presenting an exact diff; do not overwrite other brands. [Custom instructions](https://help.activecampaign.com/hc/en-us/articles/25143083381532-Active-Intelligence-custom-instructions) apply account-wide and require admin configuration. Instructions guide AI; they are not technical permission enforcement |
| First, after evidence mapping | Propose **Free Member Activation** as the Business Goal attached to 527: separate verified profile-complete, first-calculation and combined-onboarding conversions. Track signup cohorts and time to first value in existing OS reporting | [Business Goals](https://help.activecampaign.com/hc/en-us/articles/19753192812188-Business-Goals-overview) support up to four conversions per goal; an automation can have only one goal. [Automation tracking](https://help.activecampaign.com/hc/en-us/articles/25010670955932-Measure-your-automations-with-conversions-and-business-goals) uses entrants as its conversion-rate denominator. Do not substitute clicks/tag presence for missing verified events; use approved source projections |
| Next conversion phase | Propose a separate **Activated Free to Paid** goal for the revised conversion journey: eligible Free members to authoritative paid subscription, then separately settled Stripe payment, refunds/disputes and retention | Do not bind 513 or set targets before its approved rewrite and event mapping. AC ecommerce values never establish collected revenue. Business Goal detail windows are capped at 30 days; existing OS cohort records must cover longer conversion/retention windows |
| First measurement pass | Use BotSense and Apple Privacy filters, then compare delivery, human clicks, replies, completion and opt-outs by exact campaign ID and stable cohort | [BotSense](https://help.activecampaign.com/hc/en-us/articles/15589721423772-Introducing-BotSense) and [Apple Privacy filtering](https://help.activecampaign.com/hc/en-us/articles/5955589793564-Apple-Privacy-filter-overview) reduce misleading engagement data. Verify account settings before changing them. Keep unfiltered totals visible alongside filtered denominators; neither filter proves human intent |
| After one observed cohort | Use asset-referenced Active Intelligence analysis and native Custom Reports for step drop-off, errors, cohort differences and support friction; use the existing OS owner review for cross-system discrepancies | [Active Intelligence](https://help.activecampaign.com/hc/en-us/articles/20843404001436-Active-Intelligence-overview) can return charts and account-aware recommendations. It analyzes historical sends, so cold Notary Cafe/import cohorts must not be blended with current authenticated members. Similar tag-name substitution can occur in generated segments: verify exact IDs and conditions before use. [Custom Reports](https://help.activecampaign.com/hc/en-us/articles/360018102120-Intro-to-Custom-Reports) refresh daily, so they are not a live failure detector |
| Later, only if worthwhile | Trial reply-friction classification, targeted content variants and one controlled post-onboarding timing experiment | [AI Actions](https://help.activecampaign.com/hc/en-us/articles/21616984259100-Introducing-AI-Actions-Library) can require an Activities add-on. Sentiment/intent actions classify text already stored in a field, not automatically every email reply. Any reply-body ingestion/field write requires its own scope and approval. [Predictive sending](https://help.activecampaign.com/hc/en-us/articles/20315252350876-How-to-use-predictive-sending-to-automate-send-times) uses opens, includes MPP, can defer delivery up to 24 hours and advances the contact before delivery; keep the day-0 welcome immediate |

Proposed analysis prompt after measurement is connected: **For automation 527 and campaigns 871, 873, 875 and 877 only, compare delivered, bot-filtered unique clicks, replies, unsubscribes and verified activation by signup cohort. State denominators, date window, missing data and source records. Separate cold imports from authoritative members. Identify one change most likely to improve completed onboarding; propose it without changing assets, contacts, schedules or sends.** No evidence means “unknown,” not zero.

Campaign improvements remain outcome-focused: one actionable next step, milestone-aware suppression of already completed tasks, useful profile/geography/experience/inspection-interest personalization only from verified saved inputs, and help-oriented replies. Later content/timing tests should optimize completed onboarding and settled paid conversion with unsubscribe/bounce guardrails, not choose winners from opens alone. Reuse native reports and the existing OS review before proposing another admin screen or data store. Autonomous insights, scheduled analysis, dynamic AI copy, additional channels and add-on purchases remain recommendations requiring concrete approval.

**Recommended design, pending exact implementation approval:**

- 527 starts for a verified new Outseta Free signup. Existing Free-tag addition may be reused as an AC notification signal only after verified stable identity, current Free status, affirmative consent and list subscription, initial-onboarding eligibility and entry history are mapped; it must not independently establish membership or enroll cold imports, Wix prospects, paid/trial or ambiguous records. Initial Free onboarding should not loop; any future-cycle exception requires deliberate handling rather than relaxing all entry limits.
- In 527, evaluate eligibility before each email, skip already completed tasks, and route verified full completion to a holding step **before** the authoritative signup-plus-30-day wait. Do not jump past the age gate. A still-incomplete member at day 30 stays in a silent completion hold with stop conditions, not in an automatic sales sequence. No timeout may fall through into promotion.
- Immediately before Start Automation 513, recheck full completion, age, verified Free/not-paid/not-trial state, consent/subscription, suppressions, overlapping journeys and prior enrollment. Start 513 only on success, then end 527. Because starting 513 can overlap briefly with the final action of 527, destination eligibility must recognize verified completed onboarding; requiring 527 to have already ended at that instant could reject every handoff. A bare ended-automation flag is not completion evidence.
- Replace 513's two legacy form branches with the shared authoritative handoff eligibility check. Add per-send checks and a reachable paid/stop exit from all branches; use supported Jump To/exit behavior only against trustworthy projected state. Verify once-only behavior for the 113 prior entrants and reconcile the waiting positions before resuming; changing only the start trigger does not repair already enrolled contacts.
- Replace the five-email conversion pitch with three proposed helpful messages at entry-relative days 0, 5 and 12: a relevant Pro use case, an accurate Pro/Elite comparison, then a no-pressure plan/support question. Consolidate the repeated value pitch and remove expired promotion steps from future execution while preserving historical assets. No invented discount, unlimited access, earnings/placement guarantee, personal response promise or open-based purchase-intent rule.

**Intersections:** 474 and 484 remain inactive older Free onboarding assets; do not automatically restart them. Active 478 explicitly exits and restarts 479 on tracked member-site visits, so removing a contact from 479 at 527 entry alone would not enforce suppression; 479 needs its own onboarding/current-member eligibility guard and 478's restarting behavior requires review. Active 477 still triggers on last-name changes to a plan-like text value rather than an authoritative cancellation event. It is not an approved destination for 527 exits. Routine site visits and counter 480 are engagement signals, not completed onboarding.

Coverage: scoped campaign query returned **5/5**, one page, limit 100, total 5; all five associated messages read. No member records, new exports, live edits, enrollment, activation, sends, tracking-link visits or database writes occurred.
